
===== 05-16-26-SSLG-Notice-of-Meeting (1).docx =====
May 14, 2026
NOTICE OF MEETING
TO:
ALL SUPREME SECONDARY LEARNER GOVERNMENT (SSLG) OFFICERS, S.Y. 2026-2027All Others Concerned
FROM:RHIAN JADE L. CASTROChairperson, Learner Government Coordinating Council (LGCC)President, Supreme Secondary Learner Government (SSLG)
SUBJECT:
CLUSTER IV SSLG FEDERATION LEADERSHIP TRAINING CAMP and ORGANIZATIONAL ALIGNMENT
AGENDA:
a. Preparation for the Cluster IV Leadership Training Camp
b. Initial Alignment on Organizational Expectations and Officer Conduct
c. Other Concerns
In exigency of service, this Office hereby notifies all officers of the Supreme Secondary Learner Government (SSLG) that a regular meeting has been scheduled on May 16, 2026, 8:30 A.M., at the Supreme Secondary Learner Government Office to discuss the agenda stated above to ensure proper preparation, coordination, and initial alignment of all officers.
Attendance in this meeting is strictly mandatory for all concerned members.
For inquiries, concerns, and clarifications, please contact Ms. MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOY – Chairperson, Secretariat Committee through the following contact details: Micaella Sabelle R. Monloy.

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_001.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORTSupreme Secondary Learner Government
Title of the Activity:Brigada Eskwela 2026Date:June 1-5, 2026Venue/Location:Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To prepare the school facilities for the opening of classes, ensuring a safe, clean, and conducive learning environment.
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Successfully conducted the Brigada Eskwela Kick-off Ceremony and Opening Ceremony.
School facilities and assigned areas were cleaned and prepared for the opening of classes.
Strengthened collaboration among registered clubs and organizations through the assigned cleaning tasks.
Successfully concluded the activity through a Closing Ceremony.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The Brigada Eskwela officially commenced with a Kick-off Parade followed by the Opening Ceremony. Registered clubs and organizations were deployed and assigned to designated areas within the school for the five-day clean-up drive. Participants worked together to clean and prepare the school facilities for the opening of classes. The activity concluded with a Closing Ceremony led by the organizers to recognize the efforts and participation of all volunteers.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
Limited time and varying workloads in the assigned areas required proper coordination among participating clubs and organizations.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
The assigned areas were properly coordinated among the clubs and organizations, and participants worked collaboratively to complete the clean-up tasks within the allotted schedule.
RECOMMENDATIONS
Continue strengthening coordination among participating organizations and ensure that all necessary cleaning materials are prepared before the activity to improve the implementation of future Brigada Eskwela programs.
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_002.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORT
Title of the Activity:SSLG Grade 7 Representative ElectionsDate:June 16-25, 2026 Venue/Location:Multi-Purpose Hall, Oreta GymnasiumOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To promote student leadership by facilitating the election of Grade 7 Representatives who will serve as the official spokespeople of their year level within the Supreme Secondary Learner Government (SSLG).
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Officially proclaimed the newly elected SSLG Grade 7 Representatives.
Ensured active participation of grade 7 learners in the democratic election process.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The Election of the SSLG Grade 7 Representatives was conducted in accordance with the approved election procedures. The activity commenced with the announcement of the official candidates, followed by the campaign period where they presented their platforms. A Miting de Avance was also held to introduce the candidates to the grade 7 learners. On the scheduled electionday, the voting process was successfully conducted, followed by the canvassing of votes and the official proclamation of the newly elected SSLG Grade 7 Representatives.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
The planned debate during the Miting de Avance was not conducted due to limited time brought about by the scheduled earthquake drill.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
To ensure the completion of the election program, the debate was omitted, and the activity proceeded with the candidates' campaign speeches before the conduct of the election proper.
RECOMMENDATIONS
It is recommended to schedule future meeting de avance activities on dates without conflicting school programs to provide sufficient time for all planned election activities, including the debate portion.
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_003.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORTSupreme Secondary Learner Government
Title of the Activity:Club Fair 2026Date:June 1, 2026Venue/Location:Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To foster enhanced student engagement and dedication, each student shall be given the freedom to select the club or organization they wish to participate.
To help students pick what fits their interests and goals that will also help them feel more involved in their community.
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Successfully conducted the Club Fair.
Encouraged students to join school clubs and organizations based on their interests.
Successfully organized the election of officers within the different clubs and organizations.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The Club Fair was conducted to introduce the different school clubs and organizations to the students. Each club and organization promoted its objectives and encouraged students to become members. After the membership recruitment, each club and organization conducted the election of its officers to lead their respective organizations for the school year.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
Some students had inquiries about the processes of the membership application due to insufficient instructions.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
Club advisers and officers assisted students by providing information about their respective organizations, allowing them to choose the club that best matched their interests.
RECOMMENDATIONS
Provide additional time for students to explore the different clubs and organizations and encourage each club to prepare informative presentations to help students make informed decisions
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_004.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORTSupreme Secondary Learner Government
Title of the Activity:Election of Registered School Clubs and OrganizationsDate:June 17, 2026Venue/Location:Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To promote democratic participation and student leadership by conducting a fair and transparent election of registered school clubs and organizations, ensuring that qualified student leaders are given the opportunity to represent and serve their members effectively.
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Successfully conducted the election of registered clubs and organization officers.
Ensured that each club and organization have their own set of elected officers through an organized election.
Leadership, responsibility, and active student-leader participation were promoted within the clubs and organizations.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The election of officers for registered clubs and organizations was conducted following the completion of the membership drive/registration. Each club and organization held its own election to select student-leaders who will serve as their officer for the current academic year. Club advisers supervised their respective elections to ensure proper process, and conduct.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
Some clubs experienced minor delays, due to the finalization of registered members and own specific grounds.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
Follow-up coordination and flexible timelines were implemented to ensure the completion of membership registration and related requirements.
RECOMMENDATIONS
Implement stricter facilitation of scheduled elections for club officers and provide set dates for the conduction of each election.
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_005.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORTSupreme Secondary Learner Government
Title of the Activity:Crafting of the General Plan of Action (GPOA) for Student
Organizations and ClubsDate:July 03, 2026Venue/Location:Supreme Secondary Learner Government OfficeOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To guide student organizations and clubs in collaboratively crafting a clear, organized, and goal-oriented General Plan of Action (GPOA) that encourages collaboration, strengthens leadership and responsibility, and ensures the planning of relevant and practicable activities that contribute to student's growth and the school community.
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Successfully crafted the SSLG GPOA for the current academic year.
Established meaningful programs, events, projects, and activities that will guide the SSLG in serving the student body.
Collaboration between the SSLG officers and advisers were strengthened in planning school-wide events.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The SSLG GPOA was conducted to outline the all proposed projects and programs to be implemented throughout this academic year. The proposed plans were discussed, reviewed, and refined through the participation of SSLG officers and the advisers to ensure that the activities align with the needs of the students and the goals of the school, following the current Tri-Sem calendar and tighter schedules. Suggestions and recommendations were also considered to further improve the proposed action plan before its implementation.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
Some activities were not included in the final GPOA as some were not aligned with student needs, and required more financial resources.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
The SSLG officers and advisers reviewed the proposed activities, incorporated revisions, and coordinated with one another to ensure that the GPOA is realistic, organized, and feasible for implementation.
RECOMMENDATIONS
Conduct a thorough needs assessment and feasibility review during the planning stage to ensure that all proposed activities are aligned with student needs, organizational goals, and available financial resources before their inclusion in the GPOA.
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== ACR_SSLG_2026_006.docx =====
ACTIVITY COMPLETION REPORTSupreme Secondary Learner Government
Title of the Activity:Onto the Next: Leaders’ Induction CeremonyDate:July 22, 2026Venue/Location:Multi-Purpose HallOrganizing Body:Supreme Secondary Learner GovernmentPerson-in-Charge:Amparo M. Muñoz, EdD
OBJECTIVES
To formally introduce and recognize the newly elected student leaders of all clubs and organizations, along with the new set of officers of the Parent-Teacher Association.
To instill a sense of responsibility, leadership, and commitment among the officers.
To orient student leaders on their roles, duties, and expectations throughout the school year.
To promote unity and teamwork within the student organization, particularly within the Learner Government Coordinating Council.
OUTCOMES ACHIEVED
Successfully inducted the newly elected student leaders.
Fostered collaboration among student organizations in conducting a meaningful leadership ceremony.
Inspired student leaders to uphold responsible and service-oriented leadership through the messages delivered during the program.
NARRATIVE OF ACTIVITIES
The program formally opened with an invocation, the singing of the Philippine National Anthem, and the opening remarks delivered by the School Principal. This was followed by the oath-taking ceremony administered by Hon. Noel L. Villanueva, Municipal Mayor, together with Mr. Renato M. Lindo. The Municipal Mayor also delivered an inspirational message to the newly inducted student leaders, emphasizing their responsibilities and commitment to service. The ceremony concluded with intermission performances by the Aquinian’s Pride Performing Arts and La Tigris Dance Troupe, followed by a photo opportunity with the guests and newly inducted officers.
CHALLENGES ENCOUNTERED
Minor difficulties were encountered in the physical venue setup, which required additional coordination among the assigned organizations to ensure that all preparations were completed on time.
The presentation slides used during the ceremony had font and layout issues, making some contents difficult for the audience to read from a distance.
ACTIONS TAKEN/SOLUTIONS
Strengthen coordination and collaboration among the organizations assigned to venue preparation by clearly defining responsibilities and conducting pre-event coordination meetings.
The program proceeded as scheduled despite the presentation layout issue, while the technical team maximized the available visual aids to minimize disruption to the flow of the ceremony.
RECOMMENDATIONS
Conduct a comprehensive venue preparation checklist and coordination meeting prior to the event to ensure that all logistical requirements are completed efficiently.
Standardize presentation templates with readable fonts and appropriate layouts, and conduct a projector compatibility test before the program to prevent display issues.
Hold a final technical rehearsal involving all committees to verify the functionality of audiovisual equipment, presentations, and program flow before the ceremony begins.
DOCUMENTATION
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Reviewed by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Noted by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== AP_SSLG_2026_001 (1).docx =====
ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
Onto the Next: Leaders 2026–2027 Induction Ceremony
Date: July 22, 2026Time: 8:00 A.M. to 12:00 N.N.Venue: Multi-Purpose Hall
RATIONALE:
The “Onto the Next: Leaders 2026–2027 Induction Ceremony” serves as the official commencement of the leadership journey of the newly elected officers of all recognized student clubs and organizations, as well as the elected officers of the School Parent-Teacher Association (SPTA), for the School Year 2026–2027. The ceremony formally recognizes the student leaders who have been entrusted with leading their respective organizations and empowers them to uphold the values of integrity, responsibility, and service.
Beyond the oath-taking, the activity signifies each leader’s commitment to fostering a culture of excellence, collaboration, and active student participation. It also provides an opportunity to strengthen camaraderie among student organizations, encouraging them to work together in supporting the school’s vision and in implementing meaningful programs and activities that contribute to holistic learner development.
OBJECTIVES:
Formally induct the newly elected officers of all recognized clubs and organizations for the School Year 2026–2027.
Strengthen the commitment, accountability, and sense of responsibility of leaders in fulfilling their respective roles.
Foster unity, collaboration, and camaraderie among the different student organizations.
Inspire student leaders to uphold the values of integrity, excellence, and servant leadership.
Encourage officers to actively participate in programs and initiatives that contribute to the development of the school community.
ACTIVITY:

Person/s Involved

===== AP_SSLG_2026_002.docx =====
ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
Project ECOLEAD
RATIONALE:
The Project ECOLEAD: LGCC School Clean-Up and Environmental Stewardship Program serves as a school-wide initiative for S.Y. 2026-2027 that encourages student clubs and organizations to take an active role in maintaining the cleanliness and orderliness of the school environment. Through the monthly clean-up activities, each organization will be assigned a different area to clean, allowing all clubs to take part in maintaining different parts of the school and sharing the responsibility.
The regular implementation of the activity provides students with an opportunity to practice responsibility, teamwork, and discipline in caring for their surroundings. It also encourages consistent habits in maintaining the school premises and strengthens the participation of student organizations in creating a clean, organized, and welcoming school environment.
OBJECTIVES:
Maintain the cleanliness and orderliness of the school premises through regular clean-up activities.
Encourage student clubs and organizations to actively participate in school-wide clean-up efforts.
Develop a sense of responsibility among students in caring for the school environment.
Promote teamwork and cooperation among student leaders and members through clean-up activities.
Encourage students to practice proper and consistent habits in maintaining cleanliness within the school.
ACTIVITY:
The clean-up activity shall be conducted twice a month throughout S.Y. 2026–2027, with participating student clubs and organizations working together to maintain the cleanliness and orderliness of the school premises.
BUDGET:
PHP 0.00
No budget is required for the activity. Participating clubs and organizations shall provide their own cleaning materials and supplies needed for the clean-up activities.
PERSON/S INVOLVED:
The aforementioned activity will include the following Committee Chairpersons:
School Principal IV
Head Teachers
SSLG Advisers and Co-Advisers
Club and Organization Advisers
Officers and Members of Recognized Clubs andOrganizations
Prepared by:
SAMANTHA JOY C. DE VENECIASSLG Treasurer
Noted by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser
Recommending Approval:
GILBERT F. POLICARPIOHead Teacher VI
Approved by:
AMPARO M. MUÑOZ, EDDPrincipal IV

===== AP_SSLG_2026_003.docx =====
ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
Project TINIG: Transparent Information Network for Inclusive Governance
RATIONALE:
Project TINIG: Transparent Information Network for Inclusive Governance serves as a platform for promoting transparency, accountability, and active student participation within the school community. The project provides students with access to relevant information regarding the projects, activities, reports, and financial matters of the Student Supreme Learner Government (SSLG). It also provides recognized student clubs and organizations with a space to showcase their activities, events, and contributions to the school community.
Beyond providing information, the project promotes openness and responsible governance among student leaders and organizations. It encourages greater awareness and participation among students while strengthening communication and trust between the student body, the SSLG, and other recognized student organizations. Through this initiative, Project TINIG aims to support a more informed, transparent, and inclusive student community.
OBJECTIVES:
Provide students with accessible information regarding SSLG announcements, projects, programs, activities, and other official updates.
Promote transparency and accountability by making SSLG reports, financial summaries, and other relevant records accessible to the student body.
Provide recognized student clubs and organizations with a platform to present their programs, activities, events, accomplishments, and other contributions to the school community.
Maintain an organized record of school activities and programs through reports, updates, and activity documentation.
Encourage students to stay informed and become more aware of the programs and efforts of the SSLG and other student organizations.
ACTIVITY:
Establishment and maintenance of Project TINIG as an information platform for publishing official SSLG announcements, project and activity updates, reports, financial summaries, and other relevant information for the student body.
BUDGET:
PHP 0.00
No additional budget is required for the implementation and maintenance of the project. Existing SSLG and school resources shall be utilized.
PERSON/S INVOLVED:
The aforementioned activity will include the following Committee Chairpersons:
SSLG Advisers and Co-Advisers
SSLG Officers
Prepared by:
SAMANTHA JOY C. DE VENECIASSLG Treasurer

AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser

ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
Project LIWANAG: Leading with Integrity, Witnessing Achievements, and Nurturing Advocacy for a Growth-Oriented Student Body
RATIONALE:
Project LIWANAG: Leading with Integrity, Witnessing Achievements, and Nurturing Advocacy for a Growth-Oriented Student Body is a year-long documentation initiative that aims to record and showcase the work, experiences, achievements, and growth of student leaders throughout S.Y. 2026–2027. Through a digital Legacy Journal, the project will compile meaningful activities, leadership experiences, accomplishments, and contributions of student leaders and student organizations throughout the school year.
The project aims to preserve the journey of student leadership beyond individual activities by documenting the efforts and lessons gained throughout the school year. It also provides an opportunity to recognize the contributions of student leaders and highlight how their experiences help shape their leadership and service to the school community.
Through regular documentation and quarterly features, Project LIWANAG seeks to create a lasting record of the student leaders’ work and encourage a culture of responsible leadership, service, growth, and active participation within the school community.
OBJECTIVES:
Document the activities, accomplishments, and contributions of student leaders throughout S.Y. 2026–2027.
Create a digital Legacy Journal that records the work and experiences of student leaders throughout the school year.
Recognize the efforts and achievements of student leaders and student organizations in serving the school community.
Highlight the experiences and lessons of student leaders as they carry out their roles and responsibilities.
Keep a record of the projects, activities, and achievements of student leaders that can be continued and remembered by future student leaders.
ACTIVITY:
Conduct of a quarterly digital Legacy Journal featuring the activities, accomplishments, experiences, and contributions of student leaders throughout S.Y. 2026–2027.
BUDGET:
PHP 0.00
No additional budget is needed for the project. Existing SSLG resources and digital platforms will be used.
PERSON/S INVOLVED:
The activty shall involve the following:
SSLG Governing Body/ SSLG Officers
Other School Clubs and Organizations

ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
Foundation Day 2026
RATIONALE:
The celebration of the school’s 78th Foundation Day serves as an opportunity to commemorate the school’s years of service, growth, and achievements in the school community. It brings together learners, teachers, and other members of the school community to celebrate the institution’s history and the shared experiences that have shaped it throughout the years.
The activity also aims to promote camaraderie and active participation among learners through various interactive activities and student-led booths. Through these activities, students are given an opportunity to work together, develop their entrepreneurial skills, and contribute to a meaningful and enjoyable celebration for the entire school community.
OBJECTIVES:
Celebrate the school’s 78th Foundation Day.
Promote unity and active participation among learners and members of the school community.
Provide enjoyable and interactive activities for the learners.
Develop students’ entrepreneurial and organizational skills through student-led booths.
Encourage teamwork and cooperation among members of recognized clubs and organizations.
ACTIVITY:
The activity shall include student-led booths managed by recognized clubs and organizations within the school premises. 2026 World Teachers’ Day CelebrationDate: October 5, 2026Time: 6:30 AM – 10:30 AM                                                                        Theme: SOLARE: Shining Light That Shapes Bright Futures                      Venue: Col. Jesus R. Lapus Memorial Sports Complex
RATIONALE:
The celebration of World Teachers’ Day serves as an opportunity to honor and recognize the valuable contributions of teachers to the school community. It provides a way to show appreciation for their dedication, sacrifices, and continuous efforts as mentors, guides, and role models to learners. Through this celebration, students and other members of the school community are given the opportunity to recognize and value the important role of teachers in guiding learners and supporting their growth. With the theme “SOLARE: Shining Light That Shapes Bright Futures,” the celebration also highlights the important role of teachers in guiding and inspiring learners toward a brighter future.

===== AP_SSLG_2026_007 (1).docx =====
ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration                                                 Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
Venue: Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolDate: November 17-18, 2026
RATIONALE:
The celebration of National Students’ Day is anchored on Republic Act No. 11369, otherwise known as the “National Students’ Day Act,” which declares November 17 of every year as National Students’ Day. Pursuant to Section 2 of the Act, the State recognizes the importance of instilling love of country and social responsibility among the youth. Furthermore, Section 3 recognizes the invaluable contribution of student activism to Philippine democracy and the promotion of leadership among Filipino students. In line with these provisions, the celebration provides learners with opportunities to showcase their talents, creativity, and leadership through competitions, performances, and interactive activities.
In support of Section 4 of RA No. 11369, which encourages schools to extend their full support to student-led activities during the observance of National Students’ Day, the celebration will also feature booths managed by recognized clubs and organizations. These booths will serve as platforms to promote organizational advocacies, engage fellow learners, and generate funds for future initiatives and projects, subject to school policies and approval. Through these activities, the celebration aims to empower learners, strengthen camaraderie, encourage active participation, and foster a vibrant, inclusive, and socially responsible school community.
OBJECTIVE:
This activity aims to celebrate and recognize students’ talents, creativity, and contributions through competitions, performances, and interactive activities while providing clubs and organizations with opportunities to showcase their advocacies, engage learners, and raise funds for future initiatives and projects.
ACTIVITY:
TIME
ACTIVITY
PERSON IN-CHARGE
7:00 AM – 7:30 AM
Assembly at the School Oval
Learner Government Coordinating Council (LGCC)
7:30 AM – 7:50 AM
Parade
Learner Government Coordinating Council (LGCC)
7:50 AM – 8:00 AM
Assembly at the School Gymnasium
Learner Government Coordinating Council (LGCC)

ANNEX A
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BANNER-MAKING CONTEST
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation
All officers and members of the participating club or organization are allowed to contribute to the preparation and creation of the banner.
Each recognized club and organization may submit only one (1) entry.
2. Theme
All entries must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
The banner should show how the club or organization lives out the Aquinian spirit of going beyond limits and beyond the ordinary, such as through excellence, perseverance, service, and school pride.
Participants may incorporate a short, original slogan that complements and reinforces the theme.
3. Materials and Specifications
Participants may use appropriate art materials for their banners. The use of recycled or eco-friendly materials is encouraged but not mandatory.
All entries must be made on a fabric (cloth) measuring 6 feet × 3 feet.
The name of the participating club or organization must be clearly indicated on the banner.
All materials and expenses for the banner shall be shouldered by the participating club or organization.
4. Originality
All designs must be original and must reflect the creativity and ideas of the participating organization. Plagiarism and direct copying of existing designs are prohibited.
5. Relevance and Presentation
Each banner must clearly and effectively convey the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”, through its design, illustrations, colors, and accompanying slogan, if applicable.
Banners shall be presented by the club or organization representatives during the Presentation of Club and Organization Banners at the School Gymnasium on November 17, 2026.
6. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the preparation and submission period.
7. Submission Deadline
All entries must be submitted to the designated committee on or before the specified deadline. Late submissions may be subject to disqualification.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to submit the entry within the prescribed deadline, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism or deliberate copying of another participant’s work.
Violation of the prescribed banner specifications.
Submission of inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Entries shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The banner clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
25
Creativity and Originality
The design demonstrates original thinking, creative ideas, and a distinctive concept that expresses the Aquinian spirit.
20
Visual Impact and Artistic Quality
Effective use of colors, composition, lettering, illustrations, and overall visual presentation.
20
Relevance and Clarity of Message
The design communicates the theme clearly and effectively. A short slogan may be included to strengthen the message.
15
Craftsmanship and Neatness
The banner demonstrates neat execution, careful detailing, and appropriate handling of materials.
10
Resourcefulness and Use of Materials
Materials are used effectively and appropriately. Recycled materials may be used but are not required.
5
Compliance with Mechanics
The entry follows the required 6 ft × 3 ft dimensions, indicates the name of the club or organization, is submitted on time, and complies with the contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX B
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BATTLE OF THE BANDS
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
Each band must have a minimum of three (3) and a maximum of eight (8) members, all of whom must be currently enrolled learners of the school.
A learner may be a member of only one (1) band. Guest or session players from outside the school are not allowed.
Each band must have a band name and a designated band leader who will serve as the contact person of the SSLG.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to four (4) bands and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the band name, the full name, grade, and section of every member, the name and contact details of the band leader, and the titles of the songs to be performed. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Bands that register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered band withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered band through the contact details provided. A band that does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose its slot to the next band on the waitlist.
3. Song Requirements
Each band shall perform two (2) or three (3) songs, which may be original compositions or cover songs. Original compositions are encouraged.
The song titles submitted during registration are final. Any change must be approved by the organizers.
4. Performance Time and Order
Each band is allotted a maximum of ten (10) minutes for its performance, including its introduction.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of one (1) point for every fifteen (15) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
Bands must be at the venue and ready at the time announced by the organizers. A band that is not ready when called may forfeit its slot, subject to the organizers’ discretion.
5. Instruments and Equipment
The basic sound system and microphones will be provided by the school, subject to availability.
Bands must bring their own instruments, pedals, and other accessories.
All performances must be live. Backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, and lip-syncing are not allowed.
6. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All members must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
7. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Having a member who is not a currently enrolled learner of the school, or having a member who also belongs to another band.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Performing songs or actions with inappropriate or offensive content.
Use of backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, or lip-syncing.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Musicality and Technical Execution
Accuracy of pitch, rhythm, and tempo; quality of vocals and instrumental playing; and harmony and balance among the members.
40
Stage Presence and Audience Impact
Energy, confidence, effective use of the stage, and the ability to engage the audience.
30
Creativity and Originality
Originality of the composition or arrangement and the distinctiveness of the performance. Original compositions and creative arrangements are given due credit.
15
Teamwork and Cohesion
Coordination, chemistry, and unity among the band members.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The band observes the time limit, song and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX C
PROPOSED MECHANICS
SPOKEN WORD POETRY
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
This is an individual competition. Each learner may submit only one (1) entry and perform only one (1) poem.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to three (3) participants and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the participant’s full name, grade and section, contact details, and the title of the poem. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Learners who register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered participant withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered participant through the contact details provided. A participant who does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose the slot to the next learner on the waitlist.
3. Theme
The poem must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
Participants may draw on the Aquinian spirit of perseverance, courage, excellence, service, and school pride, among other ideas that fit the theme.
4. Language
The poem may be performed in Filipino, English, or a combination of both.
5. Originality
The poem must be the participant’s own original composition. Plagiarism, copying of existing works, and the use of AI-generated text are prohibited.
6. Performance Time
Each performance must not exceed three (3) minutes.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of two (2) points for every thirty (30) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
7. Delivery Guidelines
The poem must be performed from memory. The use of scripts or notes is not allowed.
Musical accompaniment, backing tracks, and sound effects are not allowed. The performance shall rely on the participant’s voice, expression, and movement.
Simple handheld props are allowed, provided that they are safe and do not delay the performance.
A microphone will be provided by the school.
8. Content Guidelines
The poem must be appropriate for a school audience. Content that is profane, vulgar, or discriminatory against any person or institution is not allowed.
The poem must not promote violence, self-harm, or vices.
9. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All participants must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
10. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
11. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
12. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism, copying, or the use of AI-generated text.
Performing a poem with inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The poem clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
20
Content and Originality
Depth of message, originality of ideas, effective use of imagery and figures of speech, and organization of thoughts.
25
Voice and Delivery
Voice projection, diction, pacing, tone, emotion, and mastery of the piece.
25
Stage Presence and Expression
Facial expression, gestures, eye contact, movement, and connection with the audience.
15
Overall Impact
The lasting impression and emotional effect of the performance on the audience and the judges.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The participant observes the time limit, language, content, and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100

ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration                                                 Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
Venue: Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolDate: November 17-18, 2026
RATIONALE:
The celebration of National Students’ Day is anchored on Republic Act No. 11369, otherwise known as the “National Students’ Day Act,” which declares November 17 of every year as National Students’ Day. Pursuant to Section 2 of the Act, the State recognizes the importance of instilling love of country and social responsibility among the youth. Furthermore, Section 3 recognizes the invaluable contribution of student activism to Philippine democracy and the promotion of leadership among Filipino students. In line with these provisions, the celebration provides learners with opportunities to showcase their talents, creativity, and leadership through competitions, performances, and interactive activities.
In support of Section 4 of RA No. 11369, which encourages schools to extend their full support to student-led activities during the observance of National Students’ Day, the celebration will also feature booths managed by recognized clubs and organizations. These booths will serve as platforms to promote organizational advocacies, engage fellow learners, and generate funds for future initiatives and projects, subject to school policies and approval. Through these activities, the celebration aims to empower learners, strengthen camaraderie, encourage active participation, and foster a vibrant, inclusive, and socially responsible school community.
OBJECTIVE:
This activity aims to celebrate and recognize students’ talents, creativity, and contributions through competitions, performances, and interactive activities while providing clubs and organizations with opportunities to showcase their advocacies, engage learners, and raise funds for future initiatives and projects.

ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration                                                 Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
Venue: Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolDate: November 17-18, 2026
RATIONALE:
The celebration of National Students’ Day is anchored on Republic Act No. 11369, otherwise known as the “National Students’ Day Act,” which declares November 17 of every year as National Students’ Day. Pursuant to Section 2 of the Act, the State recognizes the importance of instilling love of country and social responsibility among the youth. Furthermore, Section 3 recognizes the invaluable contribution of student activism to Philippine democracy and the promotion of leadership among Filipino students. In line with these provisions, the celebration provides learners with opportunities to showcase their talents, creativity, and leadership through competitions, performances, and interactive activities.
In support of Section 4 of RA No. 11369, which encourages schools to extend their full support to student-led activities during the observance of National Students’ Day, the celebration will also feature booths managed by recognized clubs and organizations. These booths will serve as platforms to promote organizational advocacies, engage fellow learners, and generate funds for future initiatives and projects, subject to school policies and approval. Through these activities, the celebration aims to empower learners, strengthen camaraderie, encourage active participation, and foster a vibrant, inclusive, and socially responsible school community.
OBJECTIVE:
This activity aims to celebrate and recognize students’ talents, creativity, and contributions through competitions, performances, and interactive activities while providing clubs and organizations with opportunities to showcase their advocacies, engage learners, and raise funds for future initiatives and projects.
ACTIVITY:

ANNEX A
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BANNER-MAKING CONTEST
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation
All officers and members of the participating club or organization are allowed to contribute to the preparation and creation of the banner.
Each recognized club and organization may submit only one (1) entry.
2. Theme
All entries must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
The banner should show how the club or organization lives out the Aquinian spirit of going beyond limits and beyond the ordinary, such as through excellence, perseverance, service, and school pride.
Participants may incorporate a short, original slogan that complements and reinforces the theme.
3. Materials and Specifications
Participants may use appropriate art materials for their banners. The use of recycled or eco-friendly materials is encouraged but not mandatory.
All entries must be made on a fabric (cloth) measuring 6 feet × 3 feet.
The name of the participating club or organization must be clearly indicated on the banner.
All materials and expenses for the banner shall be shouldered by the participating club or organization.
4. Originality
All designs must be original and must reflect the creativity and ideas of the participating organization. Plagiarism and direct copying of existing designs are prohibited.
5. Relevance and Presentation
Each banner must clearly and effectively convey the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”, through its design, illustrations, colors, and accompanying slogan, if applicable.
Banners shall be presented by the club or organization representatives during the Presentation of Club and Organization Banners at the School Gymnasium on November 17, 2026.
6. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the preparation and submission period.
7. Submission Deadline
All entries must be submitted to the designated committee on or before the specified deadline. Late submissions may be subject to disqualification.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to submit the entry within the prescribed deadline, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism or deliberate copying of another participant’s work.
Violation of the prescribed banner specifications.
Submission of inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Entries shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The banner clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
25
Creativity and Originality
The design demonstrates original thinking, creative ideas, and a distinctive concept that expresses the Aquinian spirit.
20
Visual Impact and Artistic Quality
Effective use of colors, composition, lettering, illustrations, and overall visual presentation.
20
Relevance and Clarity of Message
The design communicates the theme clearly and effectively. A short slogan may be included to strengthen the message.
15
Craftsmanship and Neatness
The banner demonstrates neat execution, careful detailing, and appropriate handling of materials.
10
Resourcefulness and Use of Materials
Materials are used effectively and appropriately. Recycled materials may be used but are not required.
5
Compliance with Mechanics
The entry follows the required 6 ft × 3 ft dimensions, indicates the name of the club or organization, is submitted on time, and complies with the contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX B
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BATTLE OF THE BANDS
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
Each band must have a minimum of three (3) and a maximum of eight (8) members, all of whom must be currently enrolled learners of the school.
A learner may be a member of only one (1) band. Guest or session players from outside the school are not allowed.
Each band must have a band name and a designated band leader who will serve as the contact person of the SSLG.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to four (4) bands and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the band name, the full name, grade, and section of every member, the name and contact details of the band leader, and the titles of the songs to be performed. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Bands that register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered band withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered band through the contact details provided. A band that does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose its slot to the next band on the waitlist.
3. Song Requirements
Each band shall perform two (2) or three (3) songs, which may be original compositions or cover songs. Original compositions are encouraged.
The song titles submitted during registration are final. Any change must be approved by the organizers.
4. Performance Time and Order
Each band is allotted a maximum of ten (10) minutes for its performance, including its introduction.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of one (1) point for every fifteen (15) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
Bands must be at the venue and ready at the time announced by the organizers. A band that is not ready when called may forfeit its slot, subject to the organizers’ discretion.
5. Instruments and Equipment
The basic sound system and microphones will be provided by the school, subject to availability.
Bands must bring their own instruments, pedals, and other accessories.
All performances must be live. Backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, and lip-syncing are not allowed.
6. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All members must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
7. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Having a member who is not a currently enrolled learner of the school, or having a member who also belongs to another band.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Performing songs or actions with inappropriate or offensive content.
Use of backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, or lip-syncing.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Musicality and Technical Execution
Accuracy of pitch, rhythm, and tempo; quality of vocals and instrumental playing; and harmony and balance among the members.
40
Stage Presence and Audience Impact
Energy, confidence, effective use of the stage, and the ability to engage the audience.
30
Creativity and Originality
Originality of the composition or arrangement and the distinctiveness of the performance. Original compositions and creative arrangements are given due credit.
15
Teamwork and Cohesion
Coordination, chemistry, and unity among the band members.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The band observes the time limit, song and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX C
PROPOSED MECHANICS
SPOKEN WORD POETRY
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
This is an individual competition. Each learner may submit only one (1) entry and perform only one (1) poem.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to three (3) participants and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the participant’s full name, grade and section, contact details, and the title of the poem. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Learners who register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered participant withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered participant through the contact details provided. A participant who does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose the slot to the next learner on the waitlist.
3. Theme
The poem must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
Participants may draw on the Aquinian spirit of perseverance, courage, excellence, service, and school pride, among other ideas that fit the theme.
4. Language
The poem may be performed in Filipino, English, or a combination of both.
5. Originality
The poem must be the participant’s own original composition. Plagiarism, copying of existing works, and the use of AI-generated text are prohibited.
6. Performance Time
Each performance must not exceed three (3) minutes.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of two (2) points for every thirty (30) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
7. Delivery Guidelines
The poem must be performed from memory. The use of scripts or notes is not allowed.
Musical accompaniment, backing tracks, and sound effects are not allowed. The performance shall rely on the participant’s voice, expression, and movement.
Simple handheld props are allowed, provided that they are safe and do not delay the performance.
A microphone will be provided by the school.
8. Content Guidelines
The poem must be appropriate for a school audience. Content that is profane, vulgar, or discriminatory against any person or institution is not allowed.
The poem must not promote violence, self-harm, or vices.
9. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All participants must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
10. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
11. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
12. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism, copying, or the use of AI-generated text.
Performing a poem with inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The poem clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
20
Content and Originality
Depth of message, originality of ideas, effective use of imagery and figures of speech, and organization of thoughts.
25
Voice and Delivery
Voice projection, diction, pacing, tone, emotion, and mastery of the piece.
25
Stage Presence and Expression
Facial expression, gestures, eye contact, movement, and connection with the audience.
15
Overall Impact
The lasting impression and emotional effect of the performance on the audience and the judges.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The participant observes the time limit, language, content, and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100

PROPOSED MECHANICS
SPOKEN WORD POETRY
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
This is an individual competition. Each learner may submit only one (1) entry and perform only one (1) poem.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to three (3) participants and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the participant’s full name, grade and section, contact details, and the title of the poem. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Learners who register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered participant withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered participant through the contact details provided. A participant who does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose the slot to the next learner on the waitlist.
3. Theme
The poem must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
Participants may draw on the Aquinian spirit of perseverance, courage, excellence, service, and school pride, among other ideas that fit the theme.
4. Language
The poem may be performed in Filipino, English, or a combination of both.
5. Originality
The poem must be the participant’s own original composition. Plagiarism, copying of existing works, and the use of AI-generated text are prohibited.
6. Performance Time
Each performance must not exceed three (3) minutes.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of two (2) points for every thirty (30) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
7. Delivery Guidelines
The poem must be performed from memory. The use of scripts or notes is not allowed.
Musical accompaniment, backing tracks, and sound effects are not allowed. The performance shall rely on the participant’s voice, expression, and movement.
Simple handheld props are allowed, provided that they are safe and do not delay the performance.
A microphone will be provided by the school.
8. Content Guidelines
The poem must be appropriate for a school audience. Content that is profane, vulgar, or discriminatory against any person or institution is not allowed.
The poem must not promote violence, self-harm, or vices.
9. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All participants must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
10. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
11. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
12. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism, copying, or the use of AI-generated text.
Performing a poem with inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The poem clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
20
Content and Originality
Depth of message, originality of ideas, effective use of imagery and figures of speech, and organization of thoughts.
25
Voice and Delivery
Voice projection, diction, pacing, tone, emotion, and mastery of the piece.
25
Stage Presence and Expression
Facial expression, gestures, eye contact, movement, and connection with the audience.
15
Overall Impact
The lasting impression and emotional effect of the performance on the audience and the judges.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The participant observes the time limit, language, content, and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX D
PROPOSED MECHANICS
ZUMBA
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Collaboration
The Zumba session shall be organized by the Supreme Secondary Learner Government (SSLG) in collaboration with the School Parent-Teacher Association (SPTA) Officers.
2. Schedule and Format
The Zumba session shall be held on Day 1 of the celebration, November 17, 2026, from 3:00 PM to 5:00 PM.
The activity shall be conducted as a single, one-time session.
3. Tickets and Participation
Participation in the Zumba session shall be through tickets, which will be sold by the SSLG in collaboration with the SPTA.
Only ticket holders are allowed to participate in the Zumba session. Each ticket is valid for one (1) participant only.
4. Awards
Awards shall be given to outstanding participants based on the judging criteria and rubrics in Section II. The complete list of awards is provided in Section IV.
Awardees shall receive certificates and prizes.
5. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent, comfortable, and appropriate for a school event and for physical activity.
All participants must show respect to fellow participants, organizers, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
6. Health and Safety
Participants are responsible for their own health and safety and are advised to bring drinking water.
Participants who feel unwell must stop and inform the organizers immediately.
7. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the session.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Participating without a valid ticket.
Wearing inappropriate attire or performing acts with inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act that endangers the safety of other participants.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Participants shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Energy and Enthusiasm
Level of energy, enjoyment, and positivity shown and sustained throughout the session.
30
Execution and Rhythm
Accuracy and coordination of movements, and the ability to keep with the beat and follow the routines.
30
Stamina and Consistency
Ability to sustain participation and effort from the start to the end of the session.
15
Spirit and Sportsmanship
Teamwork, encouragement of fellow participants, and the Aquinian spirit of going beyond limits and beyond the ordinary.
15
Attire and Compliance with Mechanics
Attire is decent, comfortable, and appropriate, and the participant observes the guidelines and rules of the activity.
10
TOTAL
100
III. MATERIALS NEEDED
Tickets
Certificates
Prizes
IV. LIST OF AWARDS
·  Best Male Zumba Performer                                             ·  Best in Zumba Attire
·  Best Female Zumba Performer                                         ·  Best in Zumba Facial Expression
·  Best in Zumba Stage Presence
·  Best in Zumba Dance Execution

ACTIVITY PROPOSAL
ACTIVITY TITLE:
2026 National Students’ Day CelebrationTheme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
Venue: Benigno S. Aquino National High SchoolDate: November 17–18, 2026
RATIONALE:
The celebration of National Students’ Day is anchored on Republic Act No. 11369, otherwise known as the “National Students’ Day Act,” which declares November 17 of every year as National Students’ Day. Pursuant to Section 2 of the Act, the State recognizes the importance of instilling love of country and social responsibility among the youth. Furthermore, Section 3 recognizes the invaluable contribution of student activism to Philippine democracy and the promotion of leadership among Filipino students. In line with these provisions, the celebration provides learners with opportunities to showcase their talents, creativity, and leadership through competitions, performances, and interactive activities.
In support of Section 4 of RA No. 11369, which encourages schools to extend their full support to student-led activities during the observance of National Students’ Day, the celebration will also feature booths managed by recognized clubs and organizations. These booths will serve as platforms to promote organizational advocacies, engage fellow learners, and generate funds for future initiatives and projects, subject to school policies and approval. Through these activities, the celebration aims to empower learners, strengthen camaraderie, encourage active participation, and foster a vibrant, inclusive, and socially responsible school community.
OBJECTIVE:
This activity aims to celebrate and recognize students’ talents, creativity, and contributions through competitions, performances, and interactive activities while providing clubs and organizations with opportunities to showcase their advocacies, engage learners, and raise funds for future initiatives and projects.

ANNEX A
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BANNER-MAKING CONTEST
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation
All officers and members of the participating club or organization are allowed to contribute to the preparation and creation of the banner.
Each recognized club and organization may submit only one (1) entry.
2. Theme
All entries must adhere to the official theme of the 2026 National Students’ Day Celebration, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
The banner should show how the club or organization lives out the Aquinian spirit of going beyond limits and beyond the ordinary, such as through excellence, perseverance, service, and school pride.
Participants may incorporate a short, original slogan that complements and reinforces the theme.
3. Materials and Specifications
Participants may use appropriate art materials for their banners. The use of recycled or eco-friendly materials is encouraged but not mandatory.
All entries must be made on a fabric (cloth) measuring 6 feet × 3 feet.
The name of the participating club or organization must be clearly indicated on the banner.
All materials and expenses for the banner shall be shouldered by the participating club or organization.
4. Originality
All designs must be original and must reflect the creativity and ideas of the participating organization. Plagiarism and direct copying of existing designs are prohibited.
5. Relevance and Presentation
Each banner must clearly and effectively convey the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”, through its design, illustrations, colors, and accompanying slogan, if applicable.
Banners shall be presented by the club or organization representatives during the Presentation of Club and Organization Banners at the School Gymnasium on November 17, 2026.
6. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the preparation and submission period.
7. Submission Deadline
All entries must be submitted to the designated committee on or before the specified deadline. Late submissions may be subject to disqualification.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to submit the entry within the prescribed deadline, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Plagiarism or deliberate copying of another participant’s work.
Violation of the prescribed banner specifications.
Submission of inappropriate or offensive content.
Any act of cheating or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Entries shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Adherence to Theme
The banner clearly reflects and follows the theme, “Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit”.
25
Creativity and Originality
The design demonstrates original thinking, creative ideas, and a distinctive concept that expresses the Aquinian spirit.
20
Visual Impact and Artistic Quality
Effective use of colors, composition, lettering, illustrations, and overall visual presentation.
20
Relevance and Clarity of Message
The design communicates the theme clearly and effectively. A short slogan may be included to strengthen the message.
15
Craftsmanship and Neatness
The banner demonstrates neat execution, careful detailing, and appropriate handling of materials.
10
Resourcefulness and Use of Materials
Materials are used effectively and appropriately. Recycled materials may be used but are not required.
5
Compliance with Mechanics
The entry follows the required 6 ft × 3 ft dimensions, indicates the name of the club or organization, is submitted on time, and complies with the contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100
ANNEX B
PROPOSED MECHANICS
BATTLE OF THE BANDS
2026 National Students’ Day Celebration
Theme: Beyond Limits, Beyond Ordinary: The Aquinian Spirit
I. MECHANICS
1. Participation and Eligibility
The contest is open to all currently enrolled learners of Benigno S. Aquino National High School for School Year 2026–2027.
Each band must have a minimum of three (3) and a maximum of eight (8) members, all of whom must be currently enrolled learners of the school.
A learner may be a member of only one (1) band. Guest or session players from outside the school are not allowed.
Each band must have a band name and a designated band leader who will serve as the contact person of the SSLG.
2. Online Registration (First Come, First Served)
Registration shall be done only through the official online registration form, the link of which will be posted on the official SSLG channels. Walk-in and on-site registrations will not be accepted.
Registration shall open on the date and time announced by the SSLG and shall close once all slots are filled or on the announced deadline, whichever comes first.
Slots are limited to four (4) bands and shall be given on a first come, first served basis, based on the date and time a complete registration form is submitted.
The form must include the band name, the full name, grade, and section of every member, the name and contact details of the band leader, and the titles of the songs to be performed. A registration is considered complete only when all required information has been submitted.
Bands that register after all slots are filled may be placed on a waitlist, in the order of their submission, and may take a slot only if a registered band withdraws or is disqualified.
The SSLG shall confirm each registered band through the contact details provided. A band that does not respond to the confirmation within the period set by the organizers may lose its slot to the next band on the waitlist.
3. Song Requirements
Each band shall perform two (2) or three (3) songs, which may be original compositions or cover songs. Original compositions are encouraged.
The song titles submitted during registration are final. Any change must be approved by the organizers.
4. Performance Time and Order
Each band is allotted a maximum of ten (10) minutes for its performance, including its introduction.
Exceeding the time limit shall result in a deduction of one (1) point for every fifteen (15) seconds beyond the allotted time.
The order of performance shall be determined by drawing of lots conducted by the organizers before the event.
Bands must be at the venue and ready at the time announced by the organizers. A band that is not ready when called may forfeit its slot, subject to the organizers’ discretion.
5. Instruments and Equipment
The basic sound system and microphones will be provided by the school, subject to availability.
Bands must bring their own instruments, pedals, and other accessories.
All performances must be live. Backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, and lip-syncing are not allowed.
6. Attire and Conduct
Attire must be decent and appropriate for a school event.
All members must show respect to fellow participants, judges, the audience, and school property at all times, in accordance with school policies.
7. Fair Play
All participants must observe honesty, fairness, and sportsmanship throughout the registration period and the competition.
8. Judges’ Decision
The decision of the board of judges shall be final and irrevocable.
9. Grounds for Disqualification
Failure to register through the official online registration process.
Having a member who is not a currently enrolled learner of the school, or having a member who also belongs to another band.
Failure to report when called, subject to the organizers’ rules.
Performing songs or actions with inappropriate or offensive content.
Use of backing tracks, pre-recorded vocals or instruments, or lip-syncing.
Any act of cheating, misconduct, or deliberate violation of the contest mechanics.
II. JUDGING CRITERIA AND RUBRICS
Performances shall be evaluated using the following criteria, for a total of 100 points.
Criteria
Description
Points
Musicality and Technical Execution
Accuracy of pitch, rhythm, and tempo; quality of vocals and instrumental playing; and harmony and balance among the members.
40
Stage Presence and Audience Impact
Energy, confidence, effective use of the stage, and the ability to engage the audience.
30
Creativity and Originality
Originality of the composition or arrangement and the distinctiveness of the performance. Original compositions and creative arrangements are given due credit.
15
Teamwork and Cohesion
Coordination, chemistry, and unity among the band members.
10
Compliance with Mechanics
The band observes the time limit, song and attire guidelines, and other contest rules.
5
TOTAL
100

June 23, 2026
TO:SENIOR HIGH SCHOOL TEACHERSJUNIOR HIGH SCHOOL TEACHERSAll Concerned
Greetings in the spirit of shared service and institutional excellence!
In accordance to the upcoming SSLG Grade 7 Representative Miting de Avance, a key initiative set forth in alignment with the Supreme Secondary Learner Government (SSLG) General Plan of Action (GPOA), we respectfully request to excuse the presence of the following SSLG Officers from their respective classes on June 24, 2026 from 6:30 A.M. to 9:00 A.M. Their involvement will be instrumental to the preparations necessary to ensure the event’s overall success.The time used for preparatory work will enable the student government to effectively coordinate the SSLG Grade 7 Representative Miting de Avance, ensuring that the event fulfills its mandate of fostering the holistic development of the school community. This is likewise pivotal in allowing the officers to fulfill their responsibilities to the student body.
We look forward to your favorable response to this request. May the Almighty continue to bless us.
Prepared by:
MICAELLA SABELLE R. MONLOYSSLG Secretary
Noted by:
AARON KHRISTIAN T. BANAGSSLG Adviser

June 23, 2026
TO:SENIOR HIGH SCHOOL TEACHERSJUNIOR HIGH SCHOOL TEACHERSAll Concerned
Greetings in the spirit of shared service and institutional excellence!
